# UTXO / Bitcoin Family (Plan 3 of 7) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Add the built-in UTXO family: chain `bitcoin` (mainnet, testnet, testnet4, signet, regtest) with bitcoinjs-lib over an Esplora indexer, p2wpkh/p2sh-p2wpkh/p2pkh/p2tr wallets, PSBT signing payloads with one request per input, `inputs` ordering, BIP125 replace and cancel, N-confirmation finality, block scanning, address history, the `blockstream`/`mempool` presets and the `crypto-aio/utxo` entry.

**Architecture:** An SDK-free Esplora client (`esplora.ts`) sends every REST call to the core transport under the calling port's tags (reads and proofs to the `rpc` transport, address reads to the `indexer`). Addresses, taproot tweaks, sizes, dust, coin selection, fee policy and broadcast classification are SDK-free and pure; bitcoinjs-lib does only the codec work in `codec.ts` (transaction serialization, BIP143/BIP341/legacy signature hashes, PSBT encode/decode/finalize) behind a `@noble/curves` ECC backend. The driver is tested offline against a test-only scripted Esplora node that applies Bitcoin Core's mempool rules and verifies every signature.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript 5.9.3 (CommonJS, `module`/`moduleResolution: node16`); Node ≥ 22; Jest 30 + ts-jest 29.4.12; pnpm 10.5.2; bitcoinjs-lib `7.0.2` (exact devDependency; optional peerDependency `^7.0.2`); `@noble/curves` 1.9, `@noble/hashes` 1.8, `@scure/base` 1.2 (already dependencies).

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-23-blockchain-adapter-layer-design.md`

**Also read:** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-23-plan-1-core-handoff.md` (§3 adapter obligations bind this plan), the `ChainDriver` contract table in `src/core/driver/types.ts`, Plan 2 (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-25-plan-2-evm.md`, the model for this plan's shape) and the cross-plan board `.superpowers/plan-authoring/coordination.md` in the Plan 2 checkout (rulings A5–A16 and A19–A22, lesson 18 as widened, lesson 17 final form, residuals R73 and R76, R79–R82, the phantom-success final wording and Plan 2 Task 11's `fenceGeneration` usage notes), and the pre-flight review `.superpowers/plan-authoring/preflight-plan-3.md` (C1, C2, I1, I2, M1–M16), applied throughout.

**Plan series:** Plan 1 (core, merged), Plan 2 (EVM, merged before this plan executes), **Plan 3 (this document: UTXO)**, Plans 4–6 (Tron, Solana, TON), Plan 7 (release).

## Global Constraints

- Runtime: "Node.js ≥ 22, backend only" (`engines.node >= 22`). Toolchain pinned: "TypeScript 5.9", "Jest 30 + ts-jest", "ESLint 9 flat config".
- "The core (`src/core/**`) imports no blockchain SDK; SDKs are optional peer dependencies loaded lazily." `src/core/**` must not import `src/adapters/**` or `src/testing/**`.
- "Each family ships an SDK-free **plugin** module (`src/adapters/<family>/plugin.ts`)"; "A manifest is static metadata plus `load()`. Only `load()` pulls in the adapter module and, with it, the SDK." "Lazy loading uses `require()` inside `load()`, not `import()`."
- "Hard `dependencies`: `@noble/curves`, `@noble/hashes`, `@scure/base`, `@scure/bip32`, `debug`" (plus `@scure/bip39`). No new runtime dependency.
- "Optional `peerDependencies` … All of them are also `devDependencies`, pinned to those tested versions." Here: bitcoinjs-lib `7.0.2` / `^7.0.2`.
- "If a lazy load fails because the SDK is missing ⇒ `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` with the exact install command."
- "`exports`: `.`, `./evm`, `./utxo`, … `./testing`, `./native` (CJS + `.d.ts`; `typesVersions` for older resolvers)."
- Spec §15 (UTXO): "Address types: p2wpkh (default), p2sh-p2wpkh, p2pkh, p2tr. Coin selection strategies: `accumulative` (default) and `all` (sweep); pluggable. Change goes to the wallet's change address (configurable). The ECC backend for bitcoinjs-lib is implemented over `@noble/curves` (no WASM). `prepareTransfer` exposes the PSBT (base64) for cold or hardware signing, and `submitSignatures` also accepts a signed PSBT. The indexer provider is **required**. The txid is exposed as `UnsignedTx.expectedRef` only when all inputs are witness-type."
- Spec §15 table: fee "`utxo` sat/vB × vsize; `exact` once built"; signing "1 per input: ecdsa (BIP143) or schnorr + tweak (p2tr); payload is a **PSBT**"; AttemptRef "`txid` (canonical)"; finality "N confirmations (probabilistic)"; replace/cancel "yes / yes (RBF)"; block scan "yes (Esplora block txs)"; address history "yes (Esplora)"; tokens "—"; memo "— (OP_RETURN deferred)".
- "SDKs never see real URLs or API keys; secrets stay in the transport." Every REST call sets `HttpRequest.route` to an identifier-free template (R14).
- "Drivers never hold keys and never sign." "Drivers are shared across tenants, so they hold no wallet, tenant or operation state."
- Store records hold plain data only (R11): `UnsignedTx` (the PSBT as base64), `SignedTx` (hex), orderings (`txid:vout` strings), `fee.details` (bigint and number fields) and observations never carry SDK objects.
- "Events carry only `operational`-class data." Broadcast refusal reasons are fixed literal texts with no txids, amounts or node text (R24).
- "`bigint` always means base units." Satoshis are `bigint`; fee rates are `bigint` sat/kvB.
- Every I/O call carries the tags of the `ChainDriver` contract table (purpose, retry class, quorum, quorum key, fanout, signal) (R41). No driver request path waits on a real timer; the Task 11 suite and the Task 8/9 port suites must pass 100 consecutive runs (R46).
- "Unit tests make no network calls; a guard fails any test that tries." "Integration (opt-in): `CRYPTO_AIO_INTEGRATION=1` runs read-only testnet checks." Environment variables carry flags and routing only, never keys.
- Crash tests use `env.restart({ killPrevious: true })` through `fenceGeneration` from `src/testing/generation.ts` (handoff R20, ruling A5; landed with Plan 2 Task 11), per its usage notes: the fenced clock, stores, signers and fetch per generation; a restart over the same raw parts; the killed container never closed; the clock, the stores read after a kill and the signer's counter kept raw; the kill pinned by a call on the old handle that never settles.
- Process: work on the plan's execution branch (from `main` after Plan 2 and Plan 2.5 merge, ruling A11); TDD for pure logic (red, green, commit); stage explicit paths only; never stage `.claude/`, `.superpowers/` or `.env`; never push. **Before every commit run `pnpm format && pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test`** (add `pnpm doc` when docs or exports change). Prettier may reflow code copied from this plan; that is expected.
- Every commit message ends with the trailer `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>`.

## Review Focus

These five inputs are the most likely to bite a real user, and no spec example exercises them. Each has a pinned test in the task named.

1. **A cold signer returns a PSBT whose outputs or fee were changed** (by a compromised wallet or a user's edit). It must be refused with `INVALID_INTENT` before anything is written or broadcast: only signature bytes are ever taken from a signed PSBT, and the unsigned transaction must be byte-identical. Pinned in Task 5 ("refuses a tampered transaction, another sighash type and junk") and Task 11 ("refuses a PSBT whose outputs were changed, and writes nothing").
2. **An Esplora endpoint that lags, or over-reports its head within the lag tolerance.** Finality and proofs must decide nothing (retryable), never a false `final` and never a false proven `replaced`; an indexer that does not know a transaction must not make it "not included" (lessons 14, 16, 17). A found-but-not-final transaction decides nothing even when the monitor's previous call saw our own spend final (C1). Pinned in Task 9 ("proves inclusion only at final depth; found but not final decides nothing", "answers "not included" only for a final spend by another transaction", "decides nothing while a second endpoint has not reached the height of the fact", "never answers "not included" for a transaction the indexer does not know", "treats a stale inclusion as undecided") and Task 11 ("never proves our own final transfer replaced while a backend is behind", "decides nothing while an endpoint lags or over-reports its head").
3. **Concurrent transfers from one wallet with few outputs.** They must spend disjoint inputs; the one that finds nothing free fails before signing with `INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS`, never a self-double-spend; a crashed `prepared` Operation keeps its inputs from every other transfer. Pinned in Task 11 ("never lets two concurrent transfers spend the same output", "keeps the inputs of a crashed prepared Operation reserved").
4. **A fee override typo (`{ satPerVByte: 1500n }` meant per kvB, or `1.5` as a number) or an amount that leaves dust change.** The override is parsed strictly, an absurd fee is refused before signing, and change below the dust threshold goes to the fee instead of a non-standard output. Pinned in Task 4 ("parses { satPerVByte } strictly", "refuses an absurd fee before signing", "gives change below the dust threshold to the fee") and Task 8 ("refuses what it must not build, before anything is signed").
5. **A low-fee transfer evicted from mempools** (a full mempool, expiry). Absence is never proof: the Attempt stays `pending`/`dropped`; a resend refused with "mempool min fee not met" stalls the Operation with `FEE_TOO_LOW` while it still holds its inputs; an RBF replacement then wins. Pinned in Task 11 ("stalls an evicted low-fee transfer until it is replaced, keeping its inputs").

## Decisions recorded by the plan author

The brief's scope rulings and the board's rulings bind this plan (A5: consume `fenceGeneration`; A6: `submitSignatures` accepts a signed payload, implemented in Task 1; A7: the UTXO fake is test-only; lesson 17 final form; R73; R76; R79–R82; the phantom-success final wording; A19–A22; lesson 18; the M10 ratification), as do the pre-flight review's findings (C1, C2, I1, I2, M1–M16), which the decisions below and the tasks apply. Where the brief, the board and the spec were silent, these decisions were made; each names what it costs if wrong.

- **D1. Esplora is the only data source, as two providers.** The core requires an `rpc` provider, and the manifest `requiresIndexer`. Each preset is registered as both kinds with the same base URL: the `rpc` transport serves blocks, transactions, fee estimates, spends, proofs and broadcasts; the `indexer` serves address UTXOs, balances and history. A `public` preset (mempool.space, then blockstream.info; `production: false`) is the fallback when a handle names neither; it is also the easy way to get two independent proof endpoints, since one Esplora endpoint is a proof quorum of 1 (M10, documented). The board's Plan 3 status said "No `public` preset for bitcoin"; the controller ratified this deviation (M10). Users write `{ provider: 'mempool', indexer: 'mempool' }`. Cost: two provider entries for one service.
- **D2. bitcoinjs-lib 7 is `require()`d with ES-module types.** Its package is `"type": "module"` with a CommonJS build; TypeScript refuses a CommonJS import of its types (TS1479). `sdk.ts` is the only module that loads it: `require('bitcoinjs-lib')` typed with `import type … with { 'resolution-mode': 'import' }`. Validated with `tsc`, ts-jest, ESLint, Prettier, `pnpm build` and `pnpm doc`. Cost: one indirection.
- **D3. SDK-free where it matters; bitcoinjs for codec work only.** Addresses (strict decode, derivation), the taproot tweak, output scripts, dust, transaction sizes, coin selection, fees and classification are SDK-free, so they are an implementation independent of bitcoinjs that the tests cross-check against it (lesson 11). bitcoinjs does transaction serialization, the three signature-hash algorithms and PSBTs; their correctness is pinned by BIP143 and BIP341 vectors and by byte-identical parity with bitcoinjs' own PSBT signing. Cost: two address implementations to keep in step (the tests do).
- **D4. The ECC backend is installed before every codec entry.** `useNobleEcc()` calls bitcoinjs `initEccLib` with one frozen `@noble/curves` object; bitcoinjs verifies it against its vectors once and skips a repeat install of the same object. This also undoes a `native()` caller who swapped the library-global backend. Cost: one identity check per call.
- **D5. Strict destination addresses.** Accepted: p2pkh, p2sh, p2wpkh, p2wsh, p2tr of the handle's network (HRP and base58 version bytes), bech32 only for witness v0 and bech32m only for v1, exact program lengths, all-lower or all-upper case (BIP173; normalized to lower case), never mixed, ≤ 90 characters. Refused although BIP350-valid: witness v2–v16 and v1 programs that are not 32 bytes (the library cannot know they are spendable), and a p2tr program that is not an x coordinate on the curve (sending there burns the coins). Errors name no address. Cost: a user cannot pay a future witness version until the library allows it.
- **D6. Wallet keys and types.** `wallet.utxo.addressType` (default `p2wpkh`) picks the type; `wallet.utxo.changeAddress` (default: the wallet's own address) must decode on the network and be derivable from the wallet's key unless the wallet opts out (`CONFIG_INVALID`, D30). The chain's schemes are `['secp256k1-ecdsa', 'secp256k1-schnorr']`, so `keys[0]` (a 33-byte compressed key) defines the address; a p2tr wallet may also be a Schnorr-only signer's 32-byte x-only key. A public key must be on the curve and never 32 bytes for a non-taproot type (R58). The builder refuses (`INVALID_INTENT`) unless the key owns the sending address. A sending address's type is read from the address (p2sh means p2sh-p2wpkh; p2wsh cannot send). M5 is documented, not enforced (A21: deferred to Plan 7): `BuildContext` carries keys, not the signer's schemes, so the builder cannot tell a watch-only p2tr wallet from one whose signer is ECDSA-only; such a signer fails at signing, before any broadcast. Cost: no multisig or script wallets (spec §20).
- **D7. Fee units and speeds.** Rates are sat/kvB bigints (Bitcoin Core's unit); fees are `ceil(rate × vsize / 1000)`. Overrides are `{ satPerVByte: bigint | string }` with at most three fractional digits; anything else is `INVALID_INTENT`, and a rate below the relay floor is `FEE_TOO_LOW`. Speeds read Esplora `/fee-estimates` at 2 (fast), 6 (normal) and 144 (slow) blocks, else the nearest faster target, never below `minRelayFee`; with no estimate at all, test networks use `params.feeFallback` (1 sat/vB) and mainnet fails with a retryable `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` (nothing is guessed). `estimateFee` is `bound: 'expected'` (the selection may change); `build` is `exact`. Cost: the speed targets are library policy.
- **D8. Relay policy defaults.** `minRelayFee` and `incrementalRelayFee` 1,000 sat/kvB, `dustRelayFee` 3,000 sat/kvB. Bitcoin Core 30 lowered the first two to 100 sat/kvB; 1,000 keeps what the library builds relayable by older nodes. Cost: a sub-1 sat/vB transaction needs a custom network plugin.
- **D9. The absurd-fee guard.** Handle options `maxFeeRate` (default 1,000,000 sat/kvB = 1,000 sat/vB) and `maxFee` (default 10,000,000 sat = 0.1 BTC, Bitcoin Core's `-maxtxfee`; at least 1 sat, M13) refuse a build with `INVALID_INTENT` before anything is signed; bitcoinjs' own extraction fee check is off. A speed's rate comes from one endpoint's estimate, so it has its own, lower cap: `maxEstimatedFeeRate` (default 200,000 sat/kvB = 200 sat/vB, M3); an estimate above it decides nothing (retryable `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`), while an explicit override is bounded by `maxFeeRate` only. Cost: a genuine fee above 1,000 sat/vB needs an explicit option, and one above 200 sat/vB an explicit rate.
- **D10. Coin selection.** `accumulative` spends the largest eligible outputs first and skips outputs worth less than their own spending fee; `all` spends every eligible output. The strategy is the handle option `coinSelection` (plain data, so it is part of the pool key; "pluggable" by name). Change below the dust threshold of the change script goes to the fee. A transaction above 400,000 weight units is refused (`INVALID_INTENT`). Sizes use worst-case signatures (72-byte ECDSA with the sighash byte, 64-byte BIP340), so a signed transaction is never larger than estimated. Cost: largest-first selection consolidates slowly.
- **D11. Unconfirmed-input policy.** Handle option `minInputConfirmations` (default 1): coin selection spends only outputs with that many confirmations; `0` allows unconfirmed outputs (a replaced or evicted parent then leaves a child that is never provably dead). Inputs an RBF replacement adds are always confirmed (BIP125 rule 2). Cost: a wallet with only unconfirmed change waits a block.
- **D12. The PSBT payload.** Version 2, lock time 0, `nSequence` `0xfffffffd` on every input (BIP125 signalling; `options.rbf: false` gives `0xfffffffe`). `witness_utxo` for segwit inputs; for p2pkh the full previous transaction (`non_witness_utxo`), whose txid, output value and output script are checked against the outpoint (a lying indexer cannot inflate a legacy input and burn the difference as fee). With the handle option `nonWitnessUtxo` (default `true`, M15), segwit v0 inputs carry their checked previous transaction too, for hardware wallets that demand it; p2tr never does. `tap_internal_key` for p2tr. No BIP32 derivation fields. Signing requests are `in:<i>`: ECDSA over the BIP143 (segwit) or legacy digest with SIGHASH_ALL for the compressed key; Schnorr over the BIP341 key-path digest with SIGHASH_DEFAULT for the tweaked output key, with `params.tweak`. Cost: one `/tx/:txid/hex` read per segwit v0 input unless the option is off (the anti-tamper check compares only the unsigned transaction, so a PSBT augmented elsewhere still round-trips).
- **D13. `expectedRef`.** For p2wpkh, p2sh-p2wpkh (with the scriptSig filled in: it is part of the txid) and p2tr the txid is fixed before signing and is `UnsignedTx.expectedRef`; `assemble` refuses (`SIGNING_FAILED`) a signed transaction with another txid. p2pkh has none. Cost: none.
- **D14. Replace and cancel (spec §8.6).** A replacement keeps every input of the Attempt it replaces and its payments, pays the new fee out of change, and adds confirmed inputs only when change cannot pay it; a cancel spends the same inputs to the change address in one output. The floor for both is the larger of "old fee + incremental relay fee × new vsize" (rules 3 and 4) and "a strictly higher fee rate" (pre-v31 rule 6; a one-transaction conflict also satisfies v31's feerate diagram). A speed or override below it is `FEE_TOO_LOW`; `buildCancel` without a fee pays exactly the floor. Every new Attempt spends a superset of the original inputs, so it conflicts with every earlier Attempt (handoff §3). **R73 check** (reworded per M16): the core's `cancelBase` picks the earlier cancel with the largest total fee. Cancels of one Attempt can differ in size when `changeAddress` changes between them, so "they all have the same vsize" does not hold. The conclusion does: every cancel is built at or above the floor over its base, so it pays a strictly higher fee **and** a strictly higher rate than the largest-fee earlier cancel. Fees therefore rise with every cancel, each cancel's base is the one before it, and rates rise transitively along the same chain; the largest-fee cancel is also the highest-rate one, and a floor over it clears every earlier cancel. No proposal is needed. The rate rule uses the base's smallest possible size (M1: `minVsize`), rounded up to sat/kvB, so it clears Bitcoin Core 30's exact comparison and 28/29's truncated one. Descendants of the replaced transaction (a recipient's CPFP) are not counted: the node then refuses with `FEE_TOO_LOW` (liveness only, documented). Cost: a replacement never re-selects inputs from scratch.
- **D15. Finality and windows.** Every network: `finality: 6 confirmations`, `defaultConfirmations: 1` (a default wait is for inclusion; credit on `final`), `reorgWindow: 24`, `maxLagBlocks` 2 on mainnet and signet, 6 on testnet and testnet4 (their minimum-difficulty rule produces bursts of blocks), the transport default on regtest. No authoritative source gives these; they are library policy. Validated as safe integers ≥ 1 (lesson 14). Cost: a user wanting fewer confirmations registers a custom network.
- **D16. Proofs (lesson 17 final form, lessons 14 and 16 sharpened, C1).** "Block h is final" is attested at its own height: the proof quorum reads `/blocks/tip/height` with the monotone key `head >= h + N − 1`, so no endpoint proposes a height. `finalizedHead` (no anchor of its own) takes one endpoint's head, trails it by `PEER_SKEW` = 2 blocks (as Plan 2), and attests that. `includedFinal` answers "not included" **only** when an input of the Attempt has a quorum-attested final spender that is neither our txid nor a malleated copy of it (D29). Everything else decides nothing (a retryable `ProviderError`): our transaction unknown, in a mempool, or in a block not yet attested final; no final spender; a spend view that names our own txid. A stale inclusion (its block is no longer the block at its height) is a retryable `PROVIDER_INCONSISTENT`. Lesson 18, widened (R85): only that definitive negative proof may answer "no". Every proof method runs under `proofRead`, so every other RPC or HTTP error (a CDN or proxy 4xx, a 401/403, any non-retryable provider error) is a retryable `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` that decides nothing, never a non-retryable error the monitor would rethrow; contradictory answers decide nothing too; `observe`'s copy check (D29) runs under the same wrapper. The cost is liveness only. The monitor's `whenAbsent` calls `slotConsumed(finalized)`, which our own final spend also satisfies, and then `includedFinal`; with this rule a load-balanced backend behind, or an endpoint-set change between the two calls, can never turn that into a proven `replaced`. This **closes core residual R76 for Bitcoin**. Cost: one extra quorum read per proof.
- **D17. `observe` reads `/tx/:txid`, never `/status`.** Esplora answers `/tx/:txid/status` with `{ confirmed: false }` for a transaction it does not know, which would look like a mempool sighting. `/tx/:txid` is a 404 then. Cost: a larger answer per poll.
- **D18. No phantom verdicts (lessons 7 and 15, the phantom-success final wording).** The board's final rule: for an intent that moves a positive amount of a token, the verdict requires a recorded transfer from the sender to the intended recipient of a positive amount, never the exact amount (fee-on-transfer tokens exist). Bitcoin has no tokens (D26), every output it builds is above dust, and a native payment needs no separate record: a transaction in a block has executed, and its txid commits to its outputs, so an included txid (ours, or our malleated copy's, whose canonical txid is ours, D29) proves that the sender's inputs paid the intended recipients the intended positive amounts (`success: true`). General decoding reports every output with an address as a `vout:<n>` transfer from the input addresses, with the amounts that actually moved; an unnamed output with value makes it `partial`. Cost: none.
- **D19. Broadcast classification (lesson 3).** Esplora returns bitcoind's error in a 400 body, as `sendrawtransaction RPC error -26: …` (Blockstream electrs) or `sendrawtransaction RPC error: {"code":-26,"message":"…"}` (mempool/electrs); both parse. `rejected` only for `CheckTransaction` reasons, consensus script failures (`mandatory-script-verify-flag-failed` before Bitcoin Core 30, `block-script-verify-flag-failed` since) and `TX decode failed`; `already-known` for `txn-already-in-mempool`, `txn-already-known`, `txn-same-nonwitness-data-in-mempool`, `Transaction outputs already in utxo set`, `Transaction already in block chain` and code −27; `FEE_TOO_LOW` for `min relay fee not met`, `mempool min fee not met` and `insufficient fee`; `TX_REFUSED` ("inputs missing or already spent") for missing or conflicting inputs; every other text `TX_REFUSED` ("the node refused the transaction"). A non-400 or ambiguous failure is rethrown as the same object. Cost: an unlisted consensus text is reported refused (the safe direction).
- **D20. The scripted Esplora node is test-only (A7).** `test/adapters/utxo/support/node.ts` applies bitcoind's `sendrawtransaction` order (already in the UTXO set, already in the mempool, then ATMP: `CheckTransaction`, standardness, inputs, values, relay and mempool minimum fees, the Bitcoin Core 28–30 replacement rules, then every signature), with bitcoind's texts in either error format. Blocks, reorgs, eviction, a dynamic mempool minimum and lagging endpoints are scripted. It decodes and verifies with bitcoinjs and `@noble/curves`, so it is not shipped in `crypto-aio/testing`. Cost: users script their own Esplora for tests.
- **D21. `submitSignatures` with a signed PSBT (A6).** `TxBuilder.signaturesFrom` accepts a base64 PSBT whose unsigned transaction equals the stored one byte for byte; from each input it takes the partial signature for the request's key, or the final witness/scriptSig stack; ECDSA must be SIGHASH_ALL and Schnorr 64 bytes (SIGHASH_DEFAULT), else `INVALID_INTENT`; the recovery bit the core needs is recomputed. Nothing else in the PSBT is used; the core verifies every signature against its stored request. Cost: a signer that finalizes into a non-standard stack is not read.
- **D22. History is confirmed-only.** `history.list` pages Esplora's `/address/:address/txs/chain` (25 per page, the last txid as the cursor), newest first. Unconfirmed deposits are visible to `mode: 'head'` scans and `getTransaction`. Cost: no mempool entries in history.
- **D23. `ext.utxo` and the native client.** `ext.utxo.listUnspent(address)` and `ext.utxo.coinSelection(request)` (a dry run, spec §5.5). `native(bc, 'bitcoinjs-lib')` returns a fresh `{ bitcoin, network, esplora(path) }` per call: the bitcoinjs module, a copy of the network parameters and a GET through the handle's transport (route `/native`). Cost: the module object itself is shared by Node's require cache (it is stateless apart from the ECC backend, D4).
- **D24. Entry point and factory shape.** `crypto-aio/utxo` exports the SDK-free types, `UTXO_PEER_DEPENDENCIES` (keyed by library name, `Readonly<Record<'bitcoinjs-lib', PeerDependency>>`, Plan 2 Task 10's final shape; the manifest lists only its own entry), `UTXO_CAPABILITIES` and the `NativeClientMap` augmentation. `sdk.ts` requires the bare peer name `'bitcoinjs-lib'`. There is no `utxoChainPlugin`: other UTXO chains are out of scope (spec §20), so R79's `<family>:<name>` namespace does not arise. The board's `<family>DriverFactory(makeClient)` shape serves families with several libraries; the UTXO family has one, so `utxoDriverFactory` lives in `driver.ts` and the manifest's `load()` requires it. The controller ratified this deviation from lesson 17's final form (M10) on one condition, which `lazy.test.ts` pins: `plugin.ts` never imports `driver.ts` statically. `setProbes` runs exactly once on each transport before any traffic (tested with a counting Proxy), and the driver never reads `network.maxLagBlocks` (R36). Cost: none.
- **D25. `maxOutputs` is 1,000, enforced.** A policy limit: 1,000 taproot outputs are about 43 kvB, far below the 100 kvB standard size. The core only reports `limits()`, so `plannedOutputs` (`spend.ts`, `MAX_OUTPUTS`) refuses more with `INVALID_INTENT` (M2). Cost: larger batches need several transfers.
- **D26. No tokens, so lessons 10 (keyed URLs) and 13 (token metadata) have no UTXO analogue.** Bitcoin has no token standard in scope (spec §15: tokens "—"); `getTokenMetadata` and `normalizeTokenRef` are absent and a token asset is `UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY`. Every preset is keyless. Cost: none.
- **D27. Guides are edited by hand**, not run through Prettier (as Plan 2 D16).
- **D28. Integration routing.** `CRYPTO_AIO_IT_UTXO_NETWORK` (default `signet`) and `CRYPTO_AIO_IT_UTXO_PROVIDER` (default `blockstream`). During authoring the test passed live on signet, testnet and mainnet through `blockstream` (signet again after M14); mempool.space was unreachable from the authoring machine. Node's `fetch` ignores `HTTPS_PROXY` unless `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1` is set. The head and the finalized height are two concurrent reads, so their gap is asserted as 5 ± 1 (M14).
- **D29. A miner-malleated p2pkh copy is our payment (C2).** A p2pkh scriptSig is outside the signature, so a miner can mine our transaction under another txid (high-s, a non-minimal or extra push, `OP_NOP`). Our scriptSigs are canonical, and a third party without the key can change only their encoding and the sign of `s` (BIP66 is consensus; the sighash byte is signed). So `canonicalTwinTxid` (Task 5) normalizes a spender's scriptSigs and recomputes its txid: equality with our txid is a SHA-256d proof of our version, lock time, outpoints, sequences and outputs; anything unparsable does not match, so there are no false positives. `isOwnCopy` (Task 7) reads the spender's bytes, keyed under a quorum on the txid they hash to, and runs only for a p2pkh sender. In `includedFinal` (Task 9) a final copy is `{ included: true, success: true, txHash: copy }` at its attested block; in `observe` (Task 7) it is observed evidence only. The Attempt's ref keeps the original txid; the observation's `txHash` is the copy, and `replacedBy` on sibling Attempts names the original id. Cost: for a p2pkh sender, one outspend and one hex read when the Attempt is not found.
- **D30. The change address must be the wallet's own (ruling A19, on M6).** Change sent to a valid but foreign address is lost funds, so `changeAddressOf` (`spend.ts`, Task 8) accepts `wallet.utxo.changeAddress` only when the wallet derives it: the sending address, or any of the four wallet types of one of `BuildContext.keys` (a bounded search, at most 4 per key). Anything else is `CONFIG_INVALID`, naming no address, before anything is signed, in every build path (estimate, build, replace, cancel). `wallet.utxo.allowExternalChangeAddress: true` is the explicit opt-out, documented in `security.md`. It is an **additive** deviation from spec §9's `utxo` option shape and a Plan 7 changelog item (Task 13 adds the bullet under `[Unreleased]`). The check runs at build time, not in `fromPublicKey`, because the core also calls `fromPublicKey` with an xpub child key (`deriveAddress`), which never derives the wallet's change address. A19's "or xpub" search needs the xpub, which today's core never passes to drivers (`walletOptionsOf(config)` carries `options`, `utxo` and `ton`). Plan 2.5's A22 adds it. Task 8 Step 4b is conditional on it: when `WalletOptions` carries the xpub, `changeAddressOf` also accepts an address the xpub derives for the sender's type at indexes 0–19 of the configured path and of the change chain `1/{index}` (at most 40 child keys, searched only after the key check fails), pinned by a test; without the xpub, a change address on the xpub chain needs the opt-out. Cost: a change address of another key needs one explicit flag.

No driver request path waits on a real timer: every Esplora call is one `Transport.http` call, and bitcoinjs-lib is never on a request path. Only `sdk.ts` loads the SDK; `codec.ts`, `builder.ts` and `driver.ts` reach it through `sdk.ts`, and the plugin and the entry never do (`dist/index.js` and `dist/adapters/utxo/index.js` load no bitcoinjs module; checked in Task 10).

### Task 1: Core prerequisite: `submitSignatures` accepts a signed payload (ruling A6) (lifted into Plan 2.5)

> **Ruling A11.** This task is self-contained and is lifted into Plan 2.5 (the core prerequisites for Plans 3–6), which runs after Plan 2 merges and before this plan. If Plan 2.5 has already landed it, skip this task: confirm that `TxBuilder.signaturesFrom`, `OperationEngine.requireOwned` and the `submitSignatures(operationId, SignatureBundle[] | RawTx)` overload exist exactly as below, and that `test/core/lifecycle/signed-payload.test.ts` passes. Every later task consumes only that API.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/core/driver/types.ts` (the `TxBuilder` interface and the contract table)
- Modify: `src/core/lifecycle/engine.ts` (`requireOwned`, after `require`)
- Modify: `src/core/blockchain/handle.ts` (the error import, `submitSignatures` and a module-level `isRawTx`)
- Test: `test/core/lifecycle/signed-payload.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `OperationEngine.require(operationId)`, `OperationEngine.assertOwnedBy(target, op)` (protected), `OperationEngine.submitSignatures(target, operationId, bundles)`, `RawTx`, `SignatureBundle`.
- Produces:
  - `TxBuilder.signaturesFrom?(unsigned: UnsignedTx, signed: RawTx): readonly SignatureBundle[]` (optional port, no I/O).
  - `OperationEngine.requireOwned(target, operationId): Promise<OperationRecord>`: `require` plus the engine's own chain, network and wallet check (`assertOwnedBy`).
  - `Blockchain.submitSignatures(operationId: string, signatures: readonly SignatureBundle[] | RawTx): Promise<Submission>`. A `RawTx` goes through the driver's `signaturesFrom` (else `UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY`); the extracted bundles then take the unchanged engine path, where the R9 orchestrator verifies each against its stored request. A Plan 7 changelog item.

**Review points:**
- The handle never trusts the signed payload: the only thing that reaches the engine is `SignatureBundle[]`, verified by the orchestrator exactly like bundles a user passes.
- M8: a value that is neither an array nor `{ encoding: 'hex' | 'base64' | 'json'; data: string }` is `INVALID_INTENT`, checked at run time, before the driver sees it.
- M8: ownership comes before extraction. An Operation of another chain, network or wallet fails with `INVALID_INTENT` through `requireOwned` (the engine's own `assertOwnedBy`), and an unknown one with `NOT_FOUND`; in both cases the driver's `signaturesFrom` is never called.
- The bundle path (`Array.isArray`) is byte-for-byte the old behaviour.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

`test/core/lifecycle/signed-payload.test.ts`:

```ts
// P3-B (ruling A6): `submitSignatures` also takes a payload signed elsewhere, whose
// signatures the driver extracts; the core verifies each one like any bundle.
import { internalsOf } from '../../../src/core/blockchain/internal';
import type { RawTx, UnsignedTx } from '../../../src/core/model/transaction';
import { localSigner } from '../../../src/core/signing/local';
import type { SignatureBundle, Signer } from '../../../src/core/signing/types';
import { fakeAddress } from '../../../src/testing/fake-chain';
import { fromHex, toHex } from '../../../src/core/util/bytes';
import { createFakeEnv, type FakeEnv } from '../../../src/testing/env';
import { ctx } from '../signing/fixtures';

/** A cold wallet over `signer`'s key (default: the env's), and its prepared transfer. */
async function prepareCold(env: FakeEnv, signer: Signer = env.signer) {
  const publicKey = await signer.getPublicKey('secp256k1-ecdsa');
  const cold = env.aio
    .scope({ wallets: { cold: { publicKey: toHex(publicKey) } } })
    .blockchain({ chain: 'fakechain', wallet: 'cold' });
  const prepared = await env.run(
    cold.prepareTransfer(
      { to: env.stranger(), amount: 7n },
      { idempotencyKey: 'cold-1' },
    ),
  );
  const signed = await signer.sign(prepared.unsigned?.signingRequests ?? [], ctx);
  if (signed.status !== 'signed') throw new Error('unreachable');
  return { cold, prepared, signatures: signed.signatures };
}

/** The payload the stand-in driver reads: JSON of hex signature bundles. */
const payloadOf = (signatures: readonly SignatureBundle[]): RawTx => ({
  encoding: 'json',
  data: JSON.stringify(signatures.map((s) => ({ ...s, bytes: toHex(s.bytes) }))),
});

/** Gives the pooled fake driver a `signaturesFrom` that records what it was given. */
async function withExtractor(env: FakeEnv, bc: FakeEnv['bc']) {
  const seen: { unsigned: UnsignedTx; signed: RawTx }[] = [];
  const { driver } = await env.run(internalsOf(bc).pooled());
  Object.assign(driver.builder, {
    signaturesFrom: (unsigned: UnsignedTx, signed: RawTx) => {
      seen.push({ unsigned, signed });
      return (
        JSON.parse(signed.data) as {
          requestId: string;
          bytes: string;
          recovery?: number;
        }[]
      ).map((s) => ({ ...s, bytes: fromHex(s.bytes) }));
    },
  });
  return seen;
}

describe('submitSignatures with a signed payload (A6)', () => {
  it('passes the stored unsigned transaction to the driver and submits what it extracts', async () => {
    const env = await createFakeEnv();
    const { cold, prepared, signatures } = await prepareCold(env);
    const seen = await withExtractor(env, cold);
    const sub = await env.run(
      cold.submitSignatures(prepared.operation.id, payloadOf(signatures)),
    );
    expect(sub.state).toBe('submitted');
    expect(seen).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(seen[0]?.unsigned.payload).toEqual(prepared.unsigned?.payload);
  });

  it('verifies every extracted signature against the stored request (R9)', async () => {
    const env = await createFakeEnv();
    const { cold, prepared, signatures } = await prepareCold(env);
    await withExtractor(env, cold);
    const forged = signatures.map((s) => ({ ...s, bytes: new Uint8Array(64).fill(1) }));
    await expect(
      env.run(cold.submitSignatures(prepared.operation.id, payloadOf(forged))),
    ).rejects.toMatchObject({ code: 'SIGNATURE_MISMATCH' });
    const op = await env.stores.operations.get('default', prepared.operation.id);
    expect(op?.attempts).toHaveLength(0);
  });

  it('refuses a signed payload on a driver that cannot read one, writing nothing', async () => {
    const env = await createFakeEnv();
    const { cold, prepared, signatures } = await prepareCold(env);
    await expect(
      env.run(cold.submitSignatures(prepared.operation.id, payloadOf(signatures))),
    ).rejects.toMatchObject({ code: 'UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY' });
    const op = await env.stores.operations.get('default', prepared.operation.id);
    expect(op?.state).toBe(prepared.operation.state);
    // Bundles still work on the same Operation.
    const sub = await env.run(cold.submitSignatures(prepared.operation.id, signatures));
    expect(sub.state).toBe('submitted');
  });

  it('refuses a payload that is not { encoding, data } (M8)', async () => {
    const env = await createFakeEnv();
    const { cold, prepared } = await prepareCold(env);
    const seen = await withExtractor(env, cold);
    for (const bad of [{ encoding: 'hex' }, { encoding: 'utf8', data: '00' }, 'abcd']) {
      await expect(
        env.run(cold.submitSignatures(prepared.operation.id, bad as unknown as RawTx)),
      ).rejects.toMatchObject({ code: 'INVALID_INTENT' });
    }
    expect(seen).toHaveLength(0);
  });

  it("checks the Operation belongs to the handle's wallet before extracting (M8)", async () => {
    const env = await createFakeEnv();
    const other = localSigner.generate({ curves: ['secp256k1'], id: 'other' }).signer;
    env.chain.fund(fakeAddress(await other.getPublicKey('secp256k1-ecdsa')), 1_000n);
    const { prepared, signatures } = await prepareCold(env, other);
    // env.bc is the main wallet: another address than the cold wallet that prepared it.
    const seen = await withExtractor(env, env.bc);
    await expect(
      env.run(env.bc.submitSignatures(prepared.operation.id, payloadOf(signatures))),
    ).rejects.toMatchObject({ code: 'INVALID_INTENT' });
    expect(seen).toHaveLength(0);
  });

  it('fails with NOT_FOUND for an unknown Operation before any extraction', async () => {
    const env = await createFakeEnv();
    const seen = await withExtractor(env, env.bc);
    await expect(
      env.run(env.bc.submitSignatures('op_missing', payloadOf([]))),
    ).rejects.toMatchObject({ code: 'NOT_FOUND' });
    expect(seen).toHaveLength(0);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Check ownership in the engine (M8)**

In `src/core/lifecycle/engine.ts`, directly after the `require(operationId)` method (before the `// ---- shared building blocks` line), add:

```ts
  /**
   * A6/M8: the Operation, checked to belong to this handle's chain, network and wallet
   * before anything reads its payload (e.g. `submitSignatures` with a signed payload).
   */
  async requireOwned(
    target: OperationTarget,
    operationId: string,
  ): Promise<OperationRecord> {
    const op = await this.require(operationId);
    this.assertOwnedBy(target, op);
    return op;
  }
```

`OperationTarget`, `OperationRecord` and the protected `assertOwnedBy` already exist in that file.

- [ ] **Step 5: Accept the payload in the handle**

In `src/core/blockchain/handle.ts`, add `StateError,` to the import from `'../errors/error'` (between `ProviderError,` and `UnsupportedCapabilityError,`), and replace

```ts
  async submitSignatures(
    operationId: string,
    signatures: readonly SignatureBundle[],
  ): Promise<Submission> {
    return this.submission(
      await this.engine().submitSignatures(await this.target(), operationId, signatures),
    );
  }
```

with

```ts
  /**
   * Completes an Operation that awaits signatures, with signature bundles or with the whole
   * payload signed elsewhere (A6), e.g. a signed PSBT: the driver extracts its signatures
   * (`TxBuilder.signaturesFrom`), and each is verified against the stored request exactly
   * like a bundle. `UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY` when the chain's driver cannot read one.
   */
  async submitSignatures(
    operationId: string,
    signatures: readonly SignatureBundle[] | RawTx,
  ): Promise<Submission> {
    const target = await this.target();
    let bundles: readonly SignatureBundle[];
    if (Array.isArray(signatures)) {
      bundles = signatures;
    } else {
      if (!isRawTx(signatures)) {
        throw new ValidationError(
          'INVALID_INTENT',
          'signatures must be signature bundles or a signed payload { encoding, data }',
        );
      }
      const { builder } = target.pooled.driver;
      if (!builder.signaturesFrom) {
        throw new UnsupportedCapabilityError(
          'UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY',
          `${this.chain} cannot read a signed payload; submit signature bundles`,
        );
      }
      // M8: ownership first, so another wallet's Operation never reaches the driver.
      const record = await this.engine().requireOwned(target, operationId);
      if (!record.unsigned) {
        throw new StateError(
          'INVALID_TRANSITION',
          `operation '${operationId}' has no transaction awaiting signatures`,
          { context: { operationId } },
        );
      }
      bundles = builder.signaturesFrom(record.unsigned, signatures);
    }
    return this.submission(
      await this.engine().submitSignatures(target, operationId, bundles),
    );
  }
```

and at the end of the file, after the class, add:

```ts
/** A6/M8: the shape of a signed payload (`RawTx`), checked at run time. */
function isRawTx(value: unknown): value is RawTx {
  if (value === null || typeof value !== 'object') return false;
  const { encoding, data } = value as { encoding?: unknown; data?: unknown };
  return (
    (encoding === 'hex' || encoding === 'base64' || encoding === 'json') &&
    typeof data === 'string'
  );
}
```

`RawTx` and `ValidationError` are already imported in `handle.ts`.

  - `presets.ts`: `UTXO_PRESETS: readonly ProviderPreset[]` (`blockstream`, `mempool`, `public`, each as `rpc` and `indexer`), frozen with each preset and URL table (R56, M13).
  - `network.ts`: `UTXO_CAPABILITIES`, `CoinSelectionStrategy = 'accumulative' | 'all'`, `UtxoNetworkConfig`, `OPTION_DEFAULTS`, `utxoNetworkConfig(chain, network, options?)` (throws `ConfigError('CONFIG_INVALID')`). The handle options are `coinSelection`, `minInputConfirmations`, `rbf`, `maxFeeRate`, `maxFee`, `maxEstimatedFeeRate` (M3, default 200,000 sat/kvB) and `nonWitnessUtxo` (M15, default `true`).
  - `crypto-aio` exports the types `UtxoAddressType`, `UtxoExt`, `UtxoFeeDetails`, `UtxoFeeOverride`, `UtxoOutputType`, `UtxoSelectionPreview`, `UtxoSelectionRequest`, `UtxoUnspent`, `UtxoWalletOptions`.

**Review points:**
- Every genesis hash, HRP and base58 version byte matches Appendix A (fund-losing if wrong: a wrong prefix builds addresses of another network).
- `BITCOIN_CHAIN` is deep-frozen (R56); explorers only where the templates are verified (not testnet4, not regtest).
- Every numeric network param and handle option is validated with `CONFIG_INVALID` (lessons 10 and 14): confirmations are safe integers ≥ 1, rates are bigints ≥ 0, `maxFeeRate` and `maxEstimatedFeeRate` are at least the relay floor, `maxFee` is at least 1 satoshi (M13: `0n` would refuse every build), `nonWitnessUtxo` is a boolean, unknown options are refused.
- Presets are keyless and `production: false`; a preset refuses a network it does not serve; the exported array, each preset and each URL table are frozen (R56, M13).
- `pnpm doc` stays green: the augmentation's `UtxoExt`/`UtxoFeeDetails` are exported from the entry in this task (R55).

      expect.objectContaining({ code: 'CONFIG_INVALID' }),
    );
  });
});

    expect(UTXO_PRESETS.every((p) => Object.isFrozen(p))).toBe(true);
  });

    for (const [name, network] of [
      ['blockstream', 'testnet4'],
      ['blockstream', 'regtest'],
      ['mempool', 'regtest'],

      );
    }
    expect(() =>
      catalog.resolve('mempool', { chain: 'ethereum', network: 'mainnet' }, 'rpc'),
    ).toThrow(expect.objectContaining({ code: 'CONFIG_INVALID' }));
  });
});

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the types**

`src/adapters/utxo/types.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * SDK-free types of the UTXO family: the `ext.utxo` API, fee details and overrides, wallet
 * options, and the Esplora wire types the driver reads (spec §15). Nothing here imports an
 * SDK, so the composition root can export these types.
 */
import type { FeeSpeed } from '../../core/model/fee';
import type { CallOptions } from '../../core/transport/types';

// R37: augment the registries through the package entry, as users do with 'crypto-aio'.
declare module '../../index' {
  interface ChainRegistry {
    bitcoin: {
      family: 'utxo';
      network: 'mainnet' | 'testnet' | 'testnet4' | 'signet' | 'regtest';
    };
  }
  interface FamilyRegistry {
    utxo: { library: 'bitcoinjs-lib'; ext: UtxoExt; fee: UtxoFeeDetails };
  }
}

/** A network's address encoding: the bech32 human-readable part and base58 version bytes. */
export interface AddressParams {
  readonly bech32: string;
  readonly pubKeyHash: number;
  readonly scriptHash: number;
}

/** The address (and so the input) type of a UTXO wallet; `p2wpkh` is the default. */
export type UtxoAddressType = 'p2wpkh' | 'p2sh-p2wpkh' | 'p2pkh' | 'p2tr';

/** Every standard output type a UTXO transfer may pay to (spec §6.4 variants). */
export type UtxoOutputType = 'p2pkh' | 'p2sh' | 'p2wpkh' | 'p2wsh' | 'p2tr';

/** `WalletConfig.utxo` (spec §9). */
export interface UtxoWalletOptions {
  readonly addressType?: UtxoAddressType;
  /**
   * Where change goes; default: the wallet's own address. It must be derivable from the
   * wallet's key (any of the four address types), unless `allowExternalChangeAddress` is set.
   */
  readonly changeAddress?: string;
  /**
   * A19: send change to a `changeAddress` the wallet's key does not derive (default `false`).
   * An additive deviation from spec §9: without it, a valid but mistyped address is refused.
   */
  readonly allowExternalChangeAddress?: boolean;
}

/** `FeeEstimate.details` of the `utxo` fee kind. */
export interface UtxoFeeDetails {
  /** The fee rate in satoshis per 1,000 virtual bytes (Bitcoin Core's unit). */
  readonly satPerKvB: bigint;
  /** Virtual size of the transaction, counting worst-case (72-byte) ECDSA signatures. */
  readonly vsize: number;
  readonly inputs: number;
  /** Outputs including change. */
  readonly outputs: number;
  /** The change amount; `0n` when there is no change output. */
  readonly change: bigint;
  /** Index of the change output, or `-1` when there is none. */
  readonly changeIndex: number;
}

/**
 * An explicit UTXO fee (`TransferIntent.fee`): satoshis per virtual byte, as a bigint or a
 * decimal string with at most three fractional digits (e.g. `'1.5'`).
 */
export type UtxoFeeOverride = { readonly satPerVByte: bigint | string };

/** An unspent output of an address, as `ext.utxo.listUnspent` reports it. */
export interface UtxoUnspent {
  /** `txid:vout`, the reservation key of this output. */
  readonly outpoint: string;
  readonly txid: string;
  readonly vout: number;
  readonly value: bigint;
  readonly confirmed: boolean;
  readonly blockHeight?: bigint;
}

export interface UtxoSelectionRequest {
  readonly from: string;
  readonly outputs: readonly { readonly to: string; readonly amount: bigint }[];
  readonly fee?: FeeSpeed | UtxoFeeOverride;
  /** Outpoints to leave out (e.g. those held by live Operations). */
  readonly exclude?: readonly string[];
}

/** A dry run of the configured coin selection. */
export interface UtxoSelectionPreview {
  /** The outputs it would spend (every eligible one when `sufficient` is `false`). */
  readonly inputs: readonly { readonly outpoint: string; readonly value: bigint }[];
  readonly fee: bigint;
  readonly satPerKvB: bigint;
  readonly vsize: number;
  readonly change: bigint;
  /** `false` when the eligible outputs cannot pay for the outputs and the fee. */
  readonly sufficient: boolean;
}

/** `bc.ext.utxo`: the UTXO family extension (spec §5.5). */
export interface UtxoExt {
  readonly utxo: {
    /** Unspent outputs of an address (indexer), confirmed first, oldest first. */
    listUnspent(address: string): Promise<readonly UtxoUnspent[]>;
    /** What the configured coin selection would pick for these outputs. Signs nothing. */
    coinSelection(request: UtxoSelectionRequest): Promise<UtxoSelectionPreview>;
  };
}

/** The transport tags every UTXO I/O call carries (R41). */
export type UtxoCallTags = Pick<
  CallOptions,
  'purpose' | 'retry' | 'quorum' | 'quorumKey' | 'fanout' | 'signal'
>;

/** Esplora's `status` object of a transaction or of a spending transaction. */
export interface EsploraStatus {
  readonly confirmed: boolean;
  readonly blockHeight?: bigint;
  readonly blockHash?: string;
  readonly blockTime?: number;
}

export interface EsploraOutput {
  readonly script: string;
  readonly type: string;
  readonly address?: string;
  readonly value: bigint;
}

export interface EsploraInput {
  readonly txid: string;
  readonly vout: number;
  readonly coinbase: boolean;
  readonly sequence: number;
  /** `undefined` for a coinbase input. */
  readonly prevout?: EsploraOutput;
}

export interface EsploraTx {
  readonly txid: string;
  readonly version: number;
  readonly locktime: number;
  readonly weight: number;
  readonly fee: bigint;
  readonly vin: readonly EsploraInput[];
  readonly vout: readonly EsploraOutput[];
  readonly status: EsploraStatus;
}

export interface EsploraBlock {
  readonly hash: string;
  readonly height: bigint;
  readonly parentHash: string;
  readonly timestamp: number;
  readonly txCount: number;
}

export interface EsploraOutspend {
  readonly spent: boolean;
  readonly txid?: string;
  readonly vin?: number;
  readonly status?: EsploraStatus;
}

export interface EsploraUtxo {
  readonly txid: string;
  readonly vout: number;
  readonly value: bigint;
  readonly status: EsploraStatus;
}

export interface EsploraAddressStats {
  readonly funded: bigint;
  readonly spent: bigint;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the chain data and the presets**

`src/adapters/utxo/chains.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * The built-in Bitcoin chain and networks (spec §2). Genesis hashes, address prefixes and
 * relay-policy numbers are verified against the sources named in the Plan 3 appendix; the
 * rest is library policy:
 * - `finality: 6 confirmations` on every network, `defaultConfirmations: 1`
 *   (`waitForConfirmation` waits for inclusion by default; credit deposits on `final`).
 * - `reorgWindow: 24` (four times the finality depth).
 * - `maxLagBlocks`: 2 on mainnet and signet, 6 on the testnets (their 20-minute
 *   minimum-difficulty rule produces bursts of blocks), the transport default on regtest.
 * - `minRelayFee` and `incrementalRelayFee` 1,000 sat/kvB (1 sat/vB): the defaults of
 *   Bitcoin Core before v30, so older nodes relay what this library builds.
 * - `feeFallback`: the rate used when a test network's estimator has no data (never on
 *   mainnet, where a missing estimate fails the read instead).
 */
import type { ChainInfo, NetworkInfo } from '../../core/model/chain';

interface NetworkSpec {
  readonly id: string;
  readonly genesis: string;
  readonly testnet: boolean;
  readonly bech32: string;
  readonly pubKeyHash: number;
  readonly scriptHash: number;
  readonly explorer?: string;
  readonly maxLagBlocks?: number;
  readonly feeFallback?: bigint;
}

const NETWORKS: readonly NetworkSpec[] = [
  {
    id: 'mainnet',
    genesis: '000000000019d6689c085ae165831e934ff763ae46a2a6c172b3f1b60a8ce26f',
    testnet: false,
    bech32: 'bc',
    pubKeyHash: 0x00,
    scriptHash: 0x05,
    explorer: 'https://blockstream.info',
    maxLagBlocks: 2,
  },
  {
    id: 'testnet',
    genesis: '000000000933ea01ad0ee984209779baaec3ced90fa3f408719526f8d77f4943',
    testnet: true,
    bech32: 'tb',
    pubKeyHash: 0x6f,
    scriptHash: 0xc4,
    explorer: 'https://blockstream.info/testnet',
    maxLagBlocks: 6,
    feeFallback: 1_000n,
  },
  {
    id: 'testnet4',
    genesis: '00000000da84f2bafbbc53dee25a72ae507ff4914b867c565be350b0da8bf043',
    testnet: true,
    bech32: 'tb',
    pubKeyHash: 0x6f,
    scriptHash: 0xc4,
    maxLagBlocks: 6,
    feeFallback: 1_000n,
  },
  {
    id: 'signet',
    genesis: '00000008819873e925422c1ff0f99f7cc9bbb232af63a077a480a3633bee1ef6',
    testnet: true,
    bech32: 'tb',
    pubKeyHash: 0x6f,
    scriptHash: 0xc4,
    explorer: 'https://blockstream.info/signet',
    maxLagBlocks: 2,
    feeFallback: 1_000n,
  },
  {
    id: 'regtest',
    genesis: '0f9188f13cb7b2c71f2a335e3a4fc328bf5beb436012afca590b1a11466e2206',
    testnet: true,
    bech32: 'bcrt',
    pubKeyHash: 0x6f,
    scriptHash: 0xc4,
    feeFallback: 1_000n,
  },
];

function network(spec: NetworkSpec): NetworkInfo {
  return {
    id: spec.id,
    identity: spec.genesis,
    testnet: spec.testnet,
    feeModel: 'utxo',
    finality: { kind: 'confirmations', confirmations: 6 },
    defaultConfirmations: 1,
    reorgWindow: 24,
    ...(spec.maxLagBlocks !== undefined ? { maxLagBlocks: spec.maxLagBlocks } : {}),
    ...(spec.explorer !== undefined
      ? {
          explorer: {
            tx: `${spec.explorer}/tx/{id}`,
            address: `${spec.explorer}/address/{address}`,
          },
        }
      : {}),
    params: {
      bech32: spec.bech32,
      pubKeyHash: spec.pubKeyHash,
      scriptHash: spec.scriptHash,
      dustRelayFee: 3_000n,
      minRelayFee: 1_000n,
      incrementalRelayFee: 1_000n,
      ...(spec.feeFallback !== undefined ? { feeFallback: spec.feeFallback } : {}),
    },
  };
}

function deepFreeze<T>(value: T): T {
  if (value !== null && typeof value === 'object' && !Object.isFrozen(value)) {
    for (const child of Object.values(value)) deepFreeze(child);
    Object.freeze(value);
  }
  return value;
}

/** R56: deep-frozen, so no caller can change a network another handle uses. */
export const BITCOIN_CHAIN: ChainInfo = deepFreeze({
  id: 'bitcoin',
  family: 'utxo',
  model: 'utxo',
  ordering: 'inputs',
  // keys[0] (the ECDSA key) defines the address; p2tr wallets also sign with Schnorr.
  schemes: ['secp256k1-ecdsa', 'secp256k1-schnorr'],
  nativeAsset: { symbol: 'BTC', decimals: 8, name: 'Bitcoin' },
  defaultNetwork: 'mainnet',
  networks: Object.fromEntries(NETWORKS.map((spec) => [spec.id, network(spec)])),
});
```

`src/adapters/utxo/presets.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * Esplora provider presets (spec §11). Each is registered twice, as an `rpc` provider
 * (blocks, transactions, broadcasts, proofs, fee estimates) and as an `indexer` (address
 * UTXOs, balances, history), because the core requires an `rpc` provider and the UTXO
 * manifest `requiresIndexer`. Only base URLs verified against the operators' own sources
 * are listed (Plan 3 appendix); a preset refuses every other network with `CONFIG_INVALID`.
 * Both services are free and rate limited, so they are marked `production: false`: for
 * production, run your own Esplora and configure it as `{ endpoints: [...] }`.
 */
import type { PresetInput, ProviderPreset } from '../../core/registry/providers';
import type { EndpointConfig } from '../../core/transport/types';

type Table = Readonly<Record<string, string>>;

/** blockstream.info: mainnet, testnet (v3) and signet. */
const BLOCKSTREAM: Table = Object.freeze({
  mainnet: 'https://blockstream.info/api',
  testnet: 'https://blockstream.info/testnet/api',
  signet: 'https://blockstream.info/signet/api',
});

/** mempool.space: mainnet, testnet (v3), testnet4 and signet. */
const MEMPOOL: Table = Object.freeze({
  mainnet: 'https://mempool.space/api',
  testnet: 'https://mempool.space/testnet/api',
  testnet4: 'https://mempool.space/testnet4/api',
  signet: 'https://mempool.space/signet/api',
});

function url(table: Table, network: string): string {
  const value = table[network];
  // Unreachable through the catalog, which asks `supports` first.
  if (value === undefined) throw new Error(`no entry for bitcoin:${network}`);
  return value;
}

function presets(
  name: string,
  endpoints: (network: string) => readonly EndpointConfig[],
  supports: (network: string) => boolean,
): ProviderPreset[] {
  return (['rpc', 'indexer'] as const).map((kind) =>
    Object.freeze<ProviderPreset>({
      name,
      kind,
      production: false,
      supports: (chain: string, network: string) =>
        chain === 'bitcoin' && supports(network),
      endpoints: (input: PresetInput) => endpoints(input.network),
    }),
  );
}

export const UTXO_PRESETS: readonly ProviderPreset[] = Object.freeze([
  ...presets(
    'blockstream',
    (network) => [{ name: 'blockstream', url: url(BLOCKSTREAM, network) }],
    (network) => BLOCKSTREAM[network] !== undefined,
  ),
  ...presets(
    'mempool',
    (network) => [{ name: 'mempool', url: url(MEMPOOL, network) }],
    (network) => MEMPOOL[network] !== undefined,
  ),
  // The fallback when a handle names no provider: both services, mempool.space first.
  ...presets(
    'public',
    (network) => [
      { name: 'mempool', url: url(MEMPOOL, network), priority: 0 },
      ...(BLOCKSTREAM[network] !== undefined
        ? [{ name: 'blockstream', url: url(BLOCKSTREAM, network), priority: 1 }]
        : []),
    ],
    (network) => MEMPOOL[network] !== undefined,
  ),
]);
```

`src/adapters/utxo/network.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * What the UTXO driver needs from a network's registry entry and from the handle's
 * `options`, validated once when a driver is created, so bad data fails with
 * `CONFIG_INVALID` instead of misbehaving (lessons 10 and 14: every number is checked).
 */
import { ConfigError } from '../../core/errors/error';
import type { Capability } from '../../core/model/capability';
import type { ChainInfo, NetworkInfo } from '../../core/model/chain';
import type { AddressParams } from './types';

/** Every capability of a UTXO network (the indexer is required, spec §15). */
export const UTXO_CAPABILITIES: readonly Capability[] = Object.freeze([
  'batch-transfer',
  'replace-fee',
  'cancel',
  'block-scan',
  'address-history',
  'hd-public-derivation',
]);

export type CoinSelectionStrategy = 'accumulative' | 'all';

export interface UtxoNetworkConfig {
  /** The genesis block hash: the endpoint identity (spec §11). */
  readonly genesisHash: string;
  readonly address: AddressParams;
  /** Finality depth N: a block with N confirmations is final. */
  readonly confirmations: number;
  /** Relay policy, in sat/kvB. */
  readonly dustRelayFee: bigint;
  readonly minRelayFee: bigint;
  readonly incrementalRelayFee: bigint;
  /** The rate used when a test network's estimator has no data; `undefined` on mainnet. */
  readonly feeFallback?: bigint;
  /** Absurd-fee guard: the highest fee rate (sat/kvB) and absolute fee (sat) ever built. */
  readonly maxFeeRate: bigint;
  readonly maxFee: bigint;
  /** M3: the highest rate (sat/kvB) taken from an endpoint's estimate; above it the estimate decides nothing. */
  readonly maxEstimatedFeeRate: bigint;
  /** D12/M15: carry each segwit v0 input's previous transaction in the PSBT (hardware wallets). */
  readonly nonWitnessUtxo: boolean;
  /** Confirmations an output needs before coin selection spends it (0 allows unconfirmed). */
  readonly minInputConfirmations: number;
  readonly coinSelection: CoinSelectionStrategy;
  /** Signal BIP125 replaceability (`nSequence` 0xfffffffd) on every input. */
  readonly rbf: boolean;
  readonly capabilities: ReadonlySet<Capability>;
}

/** Library defaults for the handle `options` (Plan 3 D-decisions). */
export const OPTION_DEFAULTS = Object.freeze({
  maxFeeRate: 1_000_000n, // 1,000 sat/vB
  maxFee: 10_000_000n, // 0.1 BTC, Bitcoin Core's -maxtxfee default
  maxEstimatedFeeRate: 200_000n, // 200 sat/vB
  nonWitnessUtxo: true,
  minInputConfirmations: 1,
  coinSelection: 'accumulative' as CoinSelectionStrategy,
  rbf: true,
});

const OPTION_KEYS = new Set([
  'maxFeeRate',
  'maxFee',
  'maxEstimatedFeeRate',
  'nonWitnessUtxo',
  'minInputConfirmations',
  'coinSelection',
  'rbf',
]);

export function utxoNetworkConfig(
  chain: ChainInfo,
  network: NetworkInfo,
  options: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>> = {},
): UtxoNetworkConfig {
  const fail = (reason: string): never => {
    throw new ConfigError(
      'CONFIG_INVALID',
      `UTXO network ${chain.id}:${network.id}: ${reason}`,
    );
  };
  const identity = network.identity;
  if (identity === undefined || !/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/.test(identity)) {
    fail('its identity must be the genesis block hash (64 lowercase hex digits)');
  }
  if (network.feeModel !== 'utxo') fail(`fee model '${network.feeModel}' is not 'utxo'`);
  const finality = network.finality;
  if (finality.kind !== 'confirmations') {
    fail(`finality '${finality.kind}' is not a UTXO policy (use 'confirmations')`);
  }
  const confirmations = (finality as { confirmations: unknown }).confirmations;
  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(confirmations) || (confirmations as number) < 1) {
    fail('finality.confirmations must be a safe integer >= 1');
  }
  const params = network.params ?? {};
  const bech32 = params.bech32;
  if (typeof bech32 !== 'string' || !/^[a-z]{1,83}$/.test(bech32)) {
    fail('params.bech32 must be a lowercase human-readable part');
  }
  const byte = (key: string): number => {
    const value = params[key];
    if (!Number.isInteger(value) || (value as number) < 0 || (value as number) > 255) {
      fail(`params.${key} must be a byte (0-255)`);
    }
    return value as number;
  };
  const pubKeyHash = byte('pubKeyHash');
  const scriptHash = byte('scriptHash');
  if (pubKeyHash === scriptHash) fail('params.pubKeyHash and scriptHash must differ');
  const rate = (
    source: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>,
    key: string,
    where: string,
  ) => {
    const value = source[key];
    if (typeof value !== 'bigint' || value < 0n)
      fail(`${where}.${key} must be a bigint >= 0`);
    return value as bigint;
  };
  const dustRelayFee = rate(params, 'dustRelayFee', 'params');
  const minRelayFee = rate(params, 'minRelayFee', 'params');
  const incrementalRelayFee = rate(params, 'incrementalRelayFee', 'params');
  const feeFallback =
    params.feeFallback === undefined ? undefined : rate(params, 'feeFallback', 'params');

  for (const key of Object.keys(options)) {
    if (!OPTION_KEYS.has(key)) fail(`unknown option '${key}'`);
  }
  const merged = { ...OPTION_DEFAULTS, ...options };
  const maxFeeRate = rate(merged, 'maxFeeRate', 'options');
  const maxFee = rate(merged, 'maxFee', 'options');
  if (maxFeeRate < minRelayFee) fail('options.maxFeeRate is below the minimum relay fee');
  if (maxFee < 1n) fail('options.maxFee must be at least 1 satoshi');
  const maxEstimatedFeeRate = rate(merged, 'maxEstimatedFeeRate', 'options');
  if (maxEstimatedFeeRate < minRelayFee) {
    fail('options.maxEstimatedFeeRate is below the minimum relay fee');
  }
  if (typeof merged.nonWitnessUtxo !== 'boolean')
    fail('options.nonWitnessUtxo must be a boolean');
  const minInputConfirmations = merged.minInputConfirmations;
  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(minInputConfirmations) || minInputConfirmations < 0) {
    fail('options.minInputConfirmations must be a safe integer >= 0');
  }
  if (merged.coinSelection !== 'accumulative' && merged.coinSelection !== 'all') {
    fail(`options.coinSelection must be 'accumulative' or 'all'`);
  }
  if (typeof merged.rbf !== 'boolean') fail('options.rbf must be a boolean');

  const capabilities = new Set<Capability>(UTXO_CAPABILITIES);
  for (const c of network.capabilities?.add ?? []) capabilities.add(c);
  for (const c of network.capabilities?.remove ?? []) capabilities.delete(c);
  return {
    genesisHash: identity as string,
    address: { bech32: bech32 as string, pubKeyHash, scriptHash },
    confirmations: confirmations as number,
    dustRelayFee,
    minRelayFee,
    incrementalRelayFee,
    ...(feeFallback !== undefined ? { feeFallback } : {}),
    maxFeeRate,
    maxFee,
    maxEstimatedFeeRate,
    nonWitnessUtxo: merged.nonWitnessUtxo,
    minInputConfirmations,
    coinSelection: merged.coinSelection,
    rbf: merged.rbf,
    capabilities,
  };
}
```

### Task 3: bitcoinjs-lib, the `@noble/curves` ECC backend and the strict address codec

**Files:**
- Modify: `package.json` (devDependency, optional peer), `pnpm-lock.yaml`
- Create: `src/adapters/utxo/sdk.ts`, `src/adapters/utxo/ecc.ts`, `src/adapters/utxo/address.ts`
- Create (test support): `test/adapters/utxo/support/vectors.ts`
- Test: `test/adapters/utxo/address.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `AddressParams`, `UtxoAddressType`, `UtxoOutputType` (Task 2); `ValidationError`, `concatBytes`; `@noble/curves` (`schnorr`, `secp256k1`), `@noble/hashes` (`sha256`, `ripemd160`), `@scure/base` (`bech32`, `bech32m`, `createBase58check`).
- Produces:
  - `sdk.ts`: `bitcoin` (the bitcoinjs-lib module), the types `Network`, `Psbt`, `Transaction`, and `useNobleEcc(): void` (D2, D4).
  - `ecc.ts`: `nobleEcc` (bitcoinjs' `TinySecp256k1Interface`: `isXOnlyPoint`, `xOnlyPointAddTweak`), `XOnlyTweakResult`.
  - `address.ts`: `DecodedAddress { type; canonical; script }`, `decodeAddress(address, params)` (throws `INVALID_ADDRESS`), `WalletAddress { address; type; script; publicKey; redeemScript?; tweak?; outputKey? }`, `walletAddress(publicKey, type, params)`, `walletTypeOf(decoded)`, `taprootTweak(internalKey)`, `outputScript(type, program)`, `isXOnlyPoint(bytes)`, `hash160(data)`, `dustThreshold(script, dustRelayFee)`; re-exports `AddressParams`.
  - Test support `vectors.ts`: `REGTEST`, `TEST_KEY`/`TEST_PUBKEY`/`TEST_XONLY`, `OTHER_KEY`/`OTHER_PUBKEY`, `testSigner(id?)`, the BIP173, BIP86 and BIP350 vectors, `NOT_ON_CURVE`.

**Review points:**
- Lesson 4 (wording per M16): every accepted address decodes to exactly one script and one canonical form. Bech32 is accepted in all-lower or all-upper case, as BIP173 allows, and normalized to lower case; mixed case is refused. HRP and version bytes of the handle's network only; bech32 vs bech32m by witness version; over-long, bad padding and off-curve taproot keys refused; no error message names the address.
- R58: `walletAddress` accepts only a 33-byte compressed key on the curve, and a 32-byte x-only key only for p2tr; never a private key or an uncompressed key.
- Lesson 11: every derivation is checked against bitcoinjs-lib `payments` for all three address parameter sets, and against BIP173 and BIP86.
- `dustThreshold` is Bitcoin Core's `GetDustThreshold` (546/540/294/330/330 at 3,000 sat/kvB).
- Only `sdk.ts` requires bitcoinjs-lib; `address.ts` and `ecc.ts` are SDK-free.

/**
 * Test keys and frozen vectors for the UTXO family. The BIP vectors are quoted from the
 * BIPs (Plan 3 appendix); everything else is cross-checked against bitcoinjs-lib in tests.
 */
import { schnorr, secp256k1 } from '@noble/curves/secp256k1';
import { sha256 } from '@noble/hashes/sha256';
import { utf8ToBytes } from '@noble/hashes/utils';
import { secret } from '../../../../src/core/secret/secret';
import { localSigner } from '../../../../src/core/signing/local';
import { toHex } from '../../../../src/core/util/bytes';

/** Address parameters of regtest (the scripted node's network). */
export const REGTEST = { bech32: 'bcrt', pubKeyHash: 0x6f, scriptHash: 0xc4 } as const;

/** A test-only key: sha256('crypto-aio/utxo test key'). Never fund it on a real network. */
export const TEST_KEY = sha256(utf8ToBytes('crypto-aio/utxo test key'));
export const TEST_PUBKEY = secp256k1.getPublicKey(TEST_KEY, true);
export const TEST_XONLY = schnorr.getPublicKey(TEST_KEY);

/** A second key (a stranger's), sha256('crypto-aio/utxo other key'). */
export const OTHER_KEY = sha256(utf8ToBytes('crypto-aio/utxo other key'));
export const OTHER_PUBKEY = secp256k1.getPublicKey(OTHER_KEY, true);

export function testSigner(id = 'hot') {
  return localSigner({ id, secp256k1: secret(toHex(TEST_KEY)) });
}

/** BIP173: the compressed generator point and its mainnet p2wpkh address. */
export const BIP173_PUBKEY =
  '0279be667ef9dcbbac55a06295ce870b07029bfcdb2dce28d959f2815b16f81798';
export const BIP173_P2WPKH = 'bc1qw508d6qejxtdg4y5r3zarvary0c5xw7kv8f3t4';

/** BIP86: m/86'/0'/0'/0/0 of the "abandon … about" mnemonic. */
export const BIP86_INTERNAL_KEY =
  'cc8a4bc64d897bddc5fbc2f670f7a8ba0b386779106cf1223c6fc5d7cd6fc115';
export const BIP86_OUTPUT_KEY =
  'a60869f0dbcf1dc659c9cecbaf8050135ea9e8cdc487053f1dc6880949dc684c';
export const BIP86_ADDRESS =
  'bc1p5cyxnuxmeuwuvkwfem96lqzszd02n6xdcjrs20cac6yqjjwudpxqkedrcr';

/** BIP350: valid addresses and their output scripts. */
export const BIP350_VALID: readonly (readonly [string, string])[] = [
  [
    'BC1QW508D6QEJXTDG4Y5R3ZARVARY0C5XW7KV8F3T4',
    '0014751e76e8199196d454941c45d1b3a323f1433bd6',

/** A bech32m v1 address whose 32-byte program (x = 5) is not a curve point. */
export const NOT_ON_CURVE =

/**
 * Well-formed addresses this library refuses on purpose: BIP350's witness versions and

`test/adapters/utxo/address.test.ts`:

```ts
import { hexToBytes } from '@noble/hashes/utils';
import { bitcoin } from '../../../src/adapters/utxo/sdk';
import {
  decodeAddress,
  dustThreshold,
  taprootTweak,
  walletAddress,
  type AddressParams,
} from '../../../src/adapters/utxo/address';
import { nobleEcc } from '../../../src/adapters/utxo/ecc';
import { toHex } from '../../../src/core/util/bytes';
import {
  BIP173_P2WPKH,
  BIP173_PUBKEY,
  BIP350_INVALID,
  BIP350_REFUSED,
  BIP350_VALID,
  BIP86_ADDRESS,
  BIP86_INTERNAL_KEY,
  BIP86_OUTPUT_KEY,
  TEST_PUBKEY,
  TEST_XONLY,
} from './support/vectors';

const MAIN: AddressParams = { bech32: 'bc', pubKeyHash: 0x00, scriptHash: 0x05 };
const TEST: AddressParams = { bech32: 'tb', pubKeyHash: 0x6f, scriptHash: 0xc4 };
const REGTEST: AddressParams = { bech32: 'bcrt', pubKeyHash: 0x6f, scriptHash: 0xc4 };
const paramsOf = (address: string) =>
  address.toLowerCase().startsWith('tb1') ? TEST : MAIN;

beforeAll(() => bitcoin.initEccLib(nobleEcc));

describe('decodeAddress (strict, lesson 4)', () => {
  it.each(BIP350_VALID)('decodes %s to its BIP350 script', (address, script) => {
    const decoded = decodeAddress(address, paramsOf(address));
    expect(toHex(decoded.script)).toBe(script);
    expect(decoded.canonical).toBe(address.toLowerCase());
  });

  it.each([...BIP350_INVALID, ...BIP350_REFUSED])('refuses %s', (address) => {
    expect(() => decodeAddress(address, paramsOf(address))).toThrow(
      expect.objectContaining({ code: 'INVALID_ADDRESS' }),
    );
  });

  it('refuses another network, whitespace and junk, naming no address', () => {
    const cases: [string, AddressParams][] = [
      [BIP173_P2WPKH, TEST],
      ['bcrt1qw508d6qejxtdg4y5r3zarvary0c5xw7kygt080', MAIN],
      ['1BgGZ9tcN4rm9KBzDn7KprQz87SZ26SAMH', TEST],
      ['mipcBbFg9gMiCh81Kj8tqqdgoZub1ZJRfn', MAIN],
      [` ${BIP173_P2WPKH}`, MAIN],
      ['', MAIN],
      ['0x1234', MAIN],
    ];
    for (const [address, params] of cases) {
      let error: unknown;
      try {
        decodeAddress(address, params);
      } catch (caught) {
        error = caught;
      }
      expect(error).toMatchObject({ code: 'INVALID_ADDRESS' });
      if (address.trim()) expect((error as Error).message).not.toContain(address.trim());
    }
  });

  it('agrees with bitcoinjs-lib on every accepted address', () => {
    const accepted = [
      ...BIP350_VALID.map(([address]) => address),
      '1BgGZ9tcN4rm9KBzDn7KprQz87SZ26SAMH',
      '3P14159f73E4gFr7JterCCQh9QjiTjiZrG',
      'mipcBbFg9gMiCh81Kj8tqqdgoZub1ZJRfn',
      '2MzQwSSnBHWHqSAqtTVQ6v47XtaisrJa1Vc',
    ];
    for (const address of accepted) {
      const params = /^(tb1|m|n|2)/i.test(address) ? TEST : MAIN;
      const network =
        params === TEST ? bitcoin.networks.testnet : bitcoin.networks.bitcoin;
      expect(toHex(decodeAddress(address, params).script)).toBe(
        toHex(bitcoin.address.toOutputScript(address, network)),
      );
    }
  });
});

describe('walletAddress (independent of bitcoinjs-lib, lesson 11)', () => {
  it('matches BIP173 and BIP86', () => {
    expect(walletAddress(hexToBytes(BIP173_PUBKEY), 'p2wpkh', MAIN).address).toBe(
      BIP173_P2WPKH,
    );
    const taproot = walletAddress(hexToBytes(BIP86_INTERNAL_KEY), 'p2tr', MAIN);
    expect(taproot.address).toBe(BIP86_ADDRESS);
    expect(toHex(taproot.outputKey as Uint8Array)).toBe(BIP86_OUTPUT_KEY);
  });

  it('matches bitcoinjs-lib payments for every wallet type and network', () => {
    const cases: [AddressParams, (typeof bitcoin.networks)['bitcoin']][] = [
      [MAIN, bitcoin.networks.bitcoin],
      [TEST, bitcoin.networks.testnet],
      [REGTEST, bitcoin.networks.regtest],
    ];
    for (const [params, network] of cases) {
      const pubkey = TEST_PUBKEY;
      expect(walletAddress(pubkey, 'p2wpkh', params).address).toBe(
        bitcoin.payments.p2wpkh({ pubkey, network }).address,
      );
      expect(walletAddress(pubkey, 'p2pkh', params).address).toBe(
        bitcoin.payments.p2pkh({ pubkey, network }).address,
      );
      expect(walletAddress(pubkey, 'p2sh-p2wpkh', params).address).toBe(
        bitcoin.payments.p2sh({
          redeem: bitcoin.payments.p2wpkh({ pubkey, network }),
          network,
        }).address,
      );
      expect(walletAddress(pubkey, 'p2tr', params).address).toBe(
        bitcoin.payments.p2tr({ internalPubkey: TEST_XONLY, network }).address,
      );
      expect(walletAddress(TEST_XONLY, 'p2tr', params).address).toBe(
        walletAddress(pubkey, 'p2tr', params).address,
      );
    }
  });

  it('refuses private-key-sized, uncompressed and off-curve keys (R58)', () => {
    const uncompressed = new Uint8Array(65).fill(4);
    const offCurve = Uint8Array.from([0x02, ...new Uint8Array(32).fill(0xff)]);
    for (const [key, type] of [
      [TEST_XONLY, 'p2wpkh'],
      [TEST_XONLY, 'p2pkh'],
      [uncompressed, 'p2pkh'],
      [offCurve, 'p2wpkh'],
      [new Uint8Array(32).fill(0xff), 'p2tr'],
    ] as const) {
      expect(() => walletAddress(key, type, MAIN)).toThrow(
        expect.objectContaining({ code: 'INVALID_ADDRESS' }),
      );
    }
  });
});

describe('the @noble/curves ECC backend (spec §15)', () => {
  it("passes bitcoinjs-lib's own verification and tweaks as BIP86 does", () => {
    expect(() => bitcoin.initEccLib(nobleEcc)).not.toThrow();
    const internal = hexToBytes(BIP86_INTERNAL_KEY);
    const { tweak } = taprootTweak(internal);
    expect(
      toHex(
        nobleEcc.xOnlyPointAddTweak(internal, tweak)?.xOnlyPubkey ?? new Uint8Array(),
      ),
    ).toBe(BIP86_OUTPUT_KEY);
    expect(nobleEcc.isXOnlyPoint(new Uint8Array(32))).toBe(false);
    expect(nobleEcc.xOnlyPointAddTweak(new Uint8Array(32), tweak)).toBeNull();
    expect(
      nobleEcc.xOnlyPointAddTweak(internal, new Uint8Array(32).fill(0xff)),
    ).toBeNull();
  });
});

describe('dustThreshold (Bitcoin Core GetDustThreshold at 3,000 sat/kvB)', () => {
  it.each([
    ['p2pkh', '76a914' + '00'.repeat(20) + '88ac', 546n],
    ['p2sh', 'a914' + '00'.repeat(20) + '87', 540n],
    ['p2wpkh', '0014' + '00'.repeat(20), 294n],
    ['p2wsh', '0020' + '00'.repeat(32), 330n],
    ['p2tr', '5120' + '00'.repeat(32), 330n],
  ])('%s is dust below %s', (_type, script, dust) => {
    expect(dustThreshold(hexToBytes(script), 3_000n)).toBe(dust);
  });
});
```
